Marketing Methods We Use: SEO: We invest in search engine optimization for long-term organic lead generation. Content Creation: We hire a videographer to consistently produce high-quality content across all social media platforms. Google LSA: Google Local Services Ads have been a strong lead source for us. Industry Associations: We join and stay active in associations aligned with our target commercial clients, such as property and facility managers. Text, Email & Voicemail Campaigns: We run strategic campaigns and always include a strong follow-up sequence to maximize conversions. Local Community Engagement: We support local causes strategically and stay active in neighborhood Facebook groups to build trust and visibility. Yard Signs: Simple, cost-effective, and highly visible, we deploy them consistently on all jobs.

What has really worked for me is treating my Google business page a social media account. I post there in the updates section, and I think it really helps with my ranking.

## 161. Yelp - share your experiences

**Author:** eddy (Contributor 2)
**Posted:** 2025-05-27
**Views:** 297 | **Likes:** 2 | **Comments:** 9
**URL:** https://community.getjobber.com/discussions/marketing-forum/yelp---share-your-experiences/3712

### Content

hey my name is eddy. my main questions is how much are you paying. pro/cons. how to succeed. i was thinking starting at about $500-$1000 per month but would love some insight before i pull the trigger

### Replies (9)

#### Reply 1

**Author:** FredHodgeJr
**Posted:** 2025-05-27

Personally, I don’t recommend using Yelp. In my experience, the lead quality tends to be low, and the return on investment just doesn’t justify the spend. One of my biggest frustrations with the platform is how they filter out legitimate 5-star reviews from actual customers while leaving negative or irrelevant ones visible. That kind of review manipulation can really hurt your online credibility despite doing great work. If you’re looking to invest in lead generation, your marketing dollars are much better spent on platforms Google or Facebook. My personal favorite is Google Local Services, it puts your business at the very top of search results, includes Google’s “Google Guaranteed” badge, and only charges you for actual, qualified leads. Plus, it builds trust quickly because people already rely on Google when looking for local pros. If you’re not already on Google Local Services, I highly recommend looking into it. It’s one of the highest-converting channels I’ve seen for local home service businesses.

#### Reply 2

**Author:** Jeremy9
**Posted:** 2025-05-27

I second this. I’ve tried Yelp twice and spent hundreds of dollars on ads. I got the leads but only a small percentage were legitimate leads. The ROI is not worth the spend. I should have a least 10 5-star reviews on Yelp but they continuously remove reviews. Highly recommend Google or Facebook for ads!

#### Reply 3

**Author:** PBurgess
**Posted:** 2025-06-27

to Jeremy9 I totally agree with Jeremy. I have spent thousands for nothing. You get tons of leads, but no leads contact you back. I am still trying to find what works. I will let you know when I do!

#### Reply 4

**Author:** judithvirag
**Posted:** 2025-05-27

BUILDER 1 We have tried Yelp and Google Ads works so much better.

#### Reply 5

**Author:** BadgerHauling
**Posted:** 2025-05-27

It was a waste of money. I even met with one of their onboarding specialists to “fine tune” my page. I personally don’t recommend it. Facebook and Google have been great. However, grassroots, old school networking and being present in your community should never be forgotten.

#### Reply 6

**Author:** OLC
**Posted:** 2025-05-27

Focus on SEO its a long term strategy.

#### Reply 7

**Author:** BHS
**Posted:** 2025-06-27

Our ROAS on Yelp for 60 days was $.88. Google LSA for that same time period was $9. The major draw back of Yelp is that you have to utilize their platform to cultivate a lead vs a direct phone call or booking page link a lead gets with Google LSA. When we chatted with Yelp about how to get a positive ROAS....crickets.

#### Reply 8

**Author:** VCMEssentials
**Posted:** 2025-06-27

I have not had a good experience with Yelp, they consistently call and email you to get you to purchase their services. I only use the free option as a way for others to find my business. I did notice that a lot of "scam clients" come from Yelp as well, potential clients asking for services. For example one potential client text me with an address to clean, and stated they were out of town but would to pay with a check. I decided to do a Google search of the property and call the real estate agency and they stated that the property was not sold and that they have no idea who the person is stating that they own the property. So be wary and take as many precautions as you can especially with platforms Yelp. This experience has now made me change some of my policies especially when it comes to first time clients.

#### Reply 9

**Author:** PBurgess
**Posted:** 2025-06-27

Stay away from Yelp, Eddy! I am still trying different avenues, but I have heard Google Local Services and Google Ads works well. Just make sure you stay on top of it. SEO is crucial. I started with very little money so I learned how to build a website on WIX, training on SEO through YouTube videos etc. It is a lot but if I did it you certainly can! Good Luck to you!

## 164. Asking for Reviews

**Author:** HGLandscapeInc (Contributor 2)
**Posted:** 2025-02-27
**Views:** 757 | **Likes:** 1 | **Comments:** 6
**URL:** https://community.getjobber.com/discussions/marketing-forum/asking-for-reviews/1102

### Content

I have always been pretty bad at asking for reviews when i finish a project and i am trying to change this moving forward. Is there a way to categorize customers by who has and has not given you a review? I will have my secretary call and email previous customers moving forward to give us a review but i want to make sure we mark this down so that we are not constantly asking the same customers. REVIEWS

### Replies (6)

#### Reply 1

**Author:** OscarGil
**Posted:** 2025-02-27

Jobber has a native (paid) feature to automatically send out reviews. We use NiceJob and integrate it with Jobber. We we close a job, NiceJob starts a campaign to ask for a review. It'll send one text and three emails over a course of the month. They stop getting communications when they click on the link I think. Or, if you don't want to pay, you just need to setup an SOP to ask the customer if they can leave you a review and immediately send them a link directly to your Google Business Profile (or wherever you want the review). Then, if they don't, then follow up in two days. And then if they don't, follow up in 5 days. Etc...

#### Reply 2

**Author:** Daichi
**Posted:** 2025-02-27

I think setting up automation is a great idea but I've found that asking for a review in person provides the highest conversion. We have "Tap Cards", and have our employees walk through the completed work with the client after each job and then ask them for a review. The client can just tap our card with there phone and it'll bring them directly to the google review link. It's worked well for us.

#### Reply 3

**Author:** WiringByron
**Posted:** 2025-02-27

If you use the NiceJob integration it will actually show you right in Jobber if the client has given you a review before. And you can choose at that point to either ask for a review in Jobber or not ask for a review.

#### Reply 4

**Author:** Excavating19
**Posted:** 2025-02-27

Good question!

#### Reply 5

**Author:** Amy
**Posted:** 2025-02-27

We are trying to ask for it multiple ways. Using the Jobber text function we have our office secretary send out a text stating the following. Thank you very much for your recent purchase from Fence Masters. We deeply value your business. If you were satisfied with your experience, we kindly ask you to take a moment to leave a review on Google to assist others in finding us. Your feedback is greatly appreciated. https://g.page/r/CSkcLUQ9j7yzEAE/review Then we also created cards with a QR code that links to leaving a google review offering money off their final invoice for a review. We haven't used these yet as we just had them designed. Also want to just test the texting version first because that saves us money. For each job we have we have a job folder and on those folders we have an area to designate if a request for a review has been made or not. A simple way for us to keep track. Hope this helps!

#### Reply 6

**Author:** walletfriendly
**Posted:** 2025-06-27

to Amy Hello Amy! These are great ideas! Could you tell me more about your "job folders," as I do not see that in Jobber; I assume you have a workaround? Thank you.

## 165. 🌟 Is Your Sales Pitch Costing You Work? Get Expert Feedback! 🌟

**Author:** AndyWeins (Contributor 2)
**Posted:** 2025-05-27
**Views:** 451 | **Likes:** 4 | **Comments:** 8
**URL:** https://community.getjobber.com/discussions/marketing-forum/%F0%9F%8C%9F-is-your-sales-pitch-costing-you-work-get-expert-feedback-%F0%9F%8C%9F/3971

### Content

Your pitch is powerful, but is it doing all it can to help you win the customer fast? I'm Andy Weins, owner of Camo Crew Responsible Junk Removal. I've built my business from side hustle to full-time success by learning how to communicate with clarity and confidence, whether it's over text, email, phone, or in person. From May 27 to June 2, I'll be hosting this Ask-an-Expert to help you sharpen your sales language and make stronger first impressions that close more work. Drop your elevator pitch, sales script, or intro message in the comments, and I'll give you feedback on: What's coming across strong What might be hurting your pitch Language tweaks that build trust and confidence faster Not sure what to post? Just tell me how you typically introduce your business or respond to a lead, and I'll help you tighten it up. Let's make sure your words are working for you, not against you. If you missed my session at Jobber Blueprint Live, "Win the Customer in the First 5 Minutes: Fast-Track Your First Impression," check out upcoming events here: jobber.com/events. BRANDING HOW TO GROW YOUR BUSINESS SALES TRAINING

### Replies (8)

#### Reply 1

**Author:** julie
**Posted:** 2025-06-27

JOBBER COMMUNITY TEAM Hey Andy, awesome webinar this morning! I got a question for you. Could you share examples of phrases that sound confident but aren’t actually helpful in building trust?

#### Reply 2

**Author:** AndyWeins
**Posted:** 2025-06-27

to julie One confident saying we use is, "not a problem" or using dismissive language when discussing someone's situation. I recently learned to lean into people's concerns to instill confidence in the perceived problem the customer was having and how we will overcome it. This validates the customer's reason for seeking an expert and you understanding the severity of the situation. Customer: This treadmill in the basement is heavy and unbearable, I don't even remember how they got it down in the basement! Me: Yeah, the heaviest ones always seem to end up in basements, we've seen it all. I'll be sure to send two of our strongest guys with the right lifting equipment to ensure we get it out safely. Do you have anything else in the basement you want us to remove while we're down there? In a situation this leaning into the emotion and vulnerability helps build trust between us and we build confidence in the customer that we can handle the project.

#### Reply 3

**Author:** julie
**Posted:** 2025-06-27

JOBBER COMMUNITY TEAM to AndyWeins Wow, I love that advice. Your example helped me see how I can change up my language to help build trust! If I were the customer, I would feel much better having someone understand my frustration and pain point without any downplay. Thanks so much!

#### Reply 4

**Author:** CleanNClear
**Posted:** 2025-06-27

I've been in the cleaning business for the past two years, but recently felt a lot more pressure and competition from other businesses in my city. How do you build value and grow as a smaller company when you have larger companies in your area?

#### Reply 5

**Author:** AndyWeins
**Posted:** 2025-06-27

to CleanNClear Do a SWOT Analysis to better understand your unique market position. Internal - Strengths / Weaknesses External - Opportunities / Strengths There is a great story about the employees of a Michelin star restaurant that went to the number one rated Michelin star restaurant in the area to see what they did best. And of course the restaurant was top of the line in all aspects except for two. They had subpar coffee and treated beer drinkers second class citizens while they treated the wine drinkers with the utmost regard. The manager of the employees then challenged the employees, "how do we deliver better where our competition falls short?". So they focused on their coffee and their beer service. They were going to serve the best coffee that filled the air with the aroma of fresh roasted beans. Whenever someone asked for beer they would come by with a beer list and a beer Sommelier (beer expert) to discuss beer pairings with meals. In this regard they started with what are the opportunities and where can we win against their competition. This all starts with the SWOT analysis. Make data driven decisions, market accordingly. A great book to better understand your unique ability is from Gair Maxwell, Big Little Legends, his tagline is without magic, it's only marketing. Also, he's Canadian ;)

#### Reply 6

**Author:** AndyWeins
**Posted:** 2025-06-27

I know that there is a 5 to 10 times return on investment with lawyers through my own personal experience. Both going through a personal divorce and a business divorce of source. A very practical way is to have someone do this speaking on your behalf. As we know, all lawyers are snakes ;). What business people consistently need are introductions and networking to other business people. A great tactic used by a lawyer I met recently was to go to lunch with me and then invite me to a Brewers game to introduce me to his other clients. Obviously this is a pretty standard networking practice, the opportunity here is to introduce them to somebody else that was in a similar situation and let them do the talking for you. That in person testimonial will be worth the few dollars and hassle spent introducing people to each other. Marketing is about what you say about yourself, branding is what other people say about you. Work on your brand.

#### Reply 7

**Author:** FridmarLaw
**Posted:** 2025-06-27

to AndyWeins Thank you, Andy, for your prompt response. Of course, Great tactic! If I may have a follow up question please, will you recommend a different approach for a proactive practice, trying to avoid issues in advance as opposed to fixing after the fact? Having testimonials of what we can do is great, but creating a shift in mentality that a "stitch in time can save nine" need more refined strategy IMO. You know, measure twice, cut once. Any additional tips or insight you can provide will be greatly appreciated :-)

## 166. Integration with SendJim

**Author:** BrandenSewell (Jobber Ambassador)
**Posted:** 2025-06-27
**Views:** 129 | **Likes:** 1 | **Comments:** 2
**URL:** https://community.getjobber.com/discussions/marketing-forum/integration-with-sendjim/4065

### Content

Hey everyone, I am wanting to automate our process for sending out a "Thank You Card" after specific milestones in our customer journey. For example, after doing an estimate, after the job is completed, and then start a sequence of Holiday post cards. Is "SendJim" the best way to do this, or is there another software that you use to do this? ADVERTISING DIGITAL MARKETING STRATEGIES EMAIL MARKETING HOW TO GROW YOUR BUSINESS PAINTING PRINTED MARKETING SALES TRAINING SEO SOCIAL MEDIA MARKETING

### Replies (2)

#### Reply 1

**Author:** CleanNClear
**Posted:** 2025-06-27

Following. Curious to learn more

#### Reply 2

**Author:** Randy_Warner
**Posted:** 2025-06-27

Hey Branden. I think SendJim is a great solution overall! According to the documentation from Jobber you can have a one-time mail campaign or a multi-step campaign. The multi-step campaign can be triggered when a job has been scheduled or when an invoice has been paid; that's it. SendJim's documentation is a bit better in that it states you can trigger a mailing based on invoice paid, job scheduled, quote sent, or quote approved which is definitely much better. Additionally you can set a "max frequency" per address which is great for recurring service providers. Based on your requirements (after doing an estimate, after the job is completed) it looks it gets you almost what you want. Alternatively you could use Zapier and SendJim. This could expand the start/trigger options for your automation. SendJim has something called a Quick Send that you could leverage as an action in your automation sequence. If you wanted to go really crazy and customize the Thank You Card with specific details from the Quote/Job using the client's name or even a custom field that you might have you could use Adhesion Connect in conjunction with Zapier and something Thankster or Handwrytten. Hope this helps as you plan for this automation. It's things these that really set companies apart and help clients remember them!

**Posted:** 2025-06-27

Social media will be your best friend for getting noticed. If you haven’t already, I recommend setting up a free Google Business Profile here: https://business.google.com/us/business-profile/. This helps your business show up in local searches and Google Maps. Next, create a Meta Business account so you can run both Facebook and Instagram from one place: https://www.facebook.com/business/help/1710077379203657. Once those are set up, start showcasing your services and doing a little "About Us" storytelling to let people connect with your mission. I’d also suggest joining local platforms Nextdoor or Angi—stick with the free listings to start. **Author:** Zzzeld (Contributor 2)
**Posted:** 2025-02-27
**Views:** 360 | **Likes:** 1 | **Comments:** 3
**URL:** https://community.getjobber.com/discussions/marketing-forum/selling-firewood-in-the-winter/1275

### Content

Im wondering about selling firewood. Anyone else do this and willing to share? ADVERTISING ARBORIST DIGITAL MARKETING STRATEGIES EMAIL MARKETING HANDYMAN HOW TO GROW YOUR BUSINESS IRRIGATION SERVICES LANDSCAPING LAWN CARE PEST CONTROL PRICING STRATEGIES RV SERVICES SNOW REMOVAL SOCIAL MEDIA MARKETING

### Replies (3)

#### Reply 1

**Author:** ryaantuttle
**Posted:** 2025-02-27

Sounds a decent idea if it's another source of income! I would reach out to Chant from Chants Daily Hustle. I know he has a lot of experience with this exact topic. You can find him on Instagram https://www.instagram.com/chants_daily_hustle/.

#### Reply 2

**Author:** 
**Posted:** 2025-02-27

502Turf We sold firewood for several years to keep employees busy during down time. We had a tree service that dropped off wood already cut to length so we only had to split and stack in IBC totes. We started selling individual ricks to customers but ended up selling to two separate stores our last few years. Delivery is a good idea instead of meeting each buyer on site. Price it dumped in a pile to save you a lot of time. We did all this on about a 1/2 acre area and used a skid, skid splitter (for large 20" plus rounds), 20 ft conveyor and 30 ton splitter. Prices will vary depending on location but in central KY we expect around $95-105 per 4x8 rick. Last year selling, we sold 375 ricks. We had a hoop shelter set up to split on rain days. The wood needs to sit about a year after splitting to be the right moisture content for burning. Firewood work covered the cost of our employees and we made a little extra money but it is hard work.

#### Reply 3

**Author:** CodyFricker
**Posted:** 2025-05-27

Great Question... I have also been thinking of do the same but Iam a small business with one or two work's as needed and wont to keep them busy in the down time... Seen as they have been loyal to me and the company from the start and we all know how hard it is to get good people in this aspect... I wont to take care of them as they have me...
